# Changelog

Everything that has happened to Personal Mythology and, from session 13, to Academy, from the first request on 22 October 2026 to now, newest first. Each entry is one working session. It is assembled from the kit's own records (`provenance.json`, the review folders and the maintainer log) and from the session transcripts. Where a session recorded the site's version number, it is given. The exact day of each step in the first, three-day session was not recorded; its entries are in order.

## Where things stand

- **Scatter figures drawn for the phone (P124, row W-BW3, 15 Nov 2026).** `kit/viz.py` draws scatter figures for phone width and `kit/verify.py` checks them.
- **P123 (15 Nov 2026): `line` figures get a phone drawing.** `line` figures now carry a phone drawing, PHONE_NARROW (258) units wide, via kit/viz.py: `line` draws `_line_draw` twice, and a label with no room falls back to "ends" in the phone drawing only; verify.py's `check_phone_lines` checks it at 320 and 390 px. The `fixes` parser of kit/thin.py now accepts `. Fix:` and a literal `\n` inside a Fix. Nothing in design/theme.css changed.
- **BW1 · Mental model filed (W-BW1, 15 Nov 2026).** Building with AI v2: reading read-bw1, status written 15 Nov, next unit BW2; 6,431 + 2,951 words, 3 figures, search on; fresh check fix 1/7/7, all applied; 8 syllabus flags raised; P107 and B1 errata applied elsewhere.

Not deployed to GitHub Pages (P119: `apcanario/academy` not in the session's repository set).

- **The object reliefs rolled out (session 61, P96; Pedro, 8 Nov 2026, 21:34, on the pilot: "Looks great, go"):** 95 of the 187 reading pages show the object their text discusses at length (Greece 33 pages and 21 outlines in use, Rome 33 and 18, Personal brand 12 and 10, Building with AI 10 and 10, Information architecture 7 and 7); 92 discuss none and show none. Each page's deciding sentence is in `design/outlines/uses-why.md`, written with `uses.json` by `kit/p96_uses.py`. The library holds 77 outlines (29 added, 52 in use, 25 spare), 49 candidates rejected with their reasons. A unit's writer names its object on the unsure list's `Object:` line, filing maps it or records none (`file-unit` §4b), a new course starts with ten outlines (`new-course` 5b), and `verify.py` checks ten in use per course and every outline in use or spare. No theme change. Artifact v134, Greece edition v11, Pages academy ec36d1d.
- **Object reliefs (session 60, P95):** a reading page whose text discusses an object at length can show it under its scope line, before the section bar: an outline from the library `design/outlines/` (48 outlines from open icon sets on GitHub: game-icons.net, CC BY 3.0; Phosphor and Tabler, MIT; each with its source, commit, credit and the texts that name it) drawn by `viz.py`'s kind `relief` as the lines of a relief map of its silhouette (from `kit/relief.py`'s distance-field contours), in the page's tone, turning and breathing as every contour drawing, captioned "<what it shows>, a stylised outline drawn as a relief from an icon" with its credit, the licence linked; each How it was built page lists its reliefs under "Illustration credits" with the licences in full. Three pilots, for Pedro's review before P96 rolls them out: Greece 4.3 (the wooden horse), Rome 1.4 (a wolf's head), Personal brand A5 (a phone). Theme 6.8; the site 15.1 MB.
- **Section separators (session 59, P94):** every section of every reading page that has a top-level paragraph ends, before its next and previous, on a horizontal topography of its paragraphs (one rise per paragraph by its words along a ridge, a contour every 5.5th of the largest, in the course's tone, the column's width at 1200:170), turning and breathing as every contour drawing, each at its own phase, still under reduced motion: 1,591 separators, 330 sections without a paragraph and so without one, 2.3 MB in all, at most 6 KB each; in "Whole unit" they stand between sections. Theme 6.7; fenced code blocks render as code and lists written under a line as lists (nine units' displayed counts follow); the site 15.1 MB, the shell 840 KB.
- **Every contour drawing in slow motion, topographies on the course homes and What's new, the tile across the site (session 58, P93):** every contour drawing turns from -3 to +3 degrees and grows from 1 to 1.06 about its centre over 120 s each way, by transform only, each at its own phase, still under reduced motion; each course home and What's new opens on a hero over a topography drawn from its data (a rise per unit by its words, planned units dashed rings; a rise per day by the words filed); every unit on every course home and every planned course is the home's tile (a history unit opens to its module rows, a markdown unit links to its reading page or its brief, the planned courses are ghosts), two columns from 768 px. Theme 6.6; the markdown course homes are fetched files; shell 789 KB.
- **The home's hero, a topography from the data (session 57, P92; its drift superseded by session 58's slow turn, its unit cards by the tile):** the Academy home opens on a type-first hero: "Academy", the four areas cycling under it in their tones (CSS only; a static line under reduced motion and for a screen reader), the lede and a quiet outlined link "What's new · IA7 · 3 Nov", over contour lines `viz.py` draws from the data (one rise per area by its words written, a contour every 22,000 words, two fixed layouts, drifting 8 x 6 px over 30 s by a transform where motion is welcome; 14 KB); 70% of a phone's screen, the first area peeking under it. The areas as sections (the name only on a phone; label, lede and count from 768 px), each course a tile with the same fields and its own static contour patch. No totals, no shelf, no Continue, no map. Theme 6.5. Artifact v126; Pages academy 0864aa9.

- **Dio and every frame of a figure (session 42, A-1: P87, P88, P89):** Cassius Dio is cited by the Loeb's book (Boissevain's division) with the traditional number of the Loeb's margin in brackets where they differ, and every history citation now follows it (`reviews/units-9-13/log.md`, P87 and P88). `verify.py --shots <dir> --page <id>` saves, for each figure of the page, its opening frame and a sheet of every frame a reader scrolls it across, half a frame apart, phone, tablet and desktop, dark and light; `p85_frames.py` is retired. Brand + AI has 14 of 22 units (A5 and B7 filed).
- **Greece and Rome in Markdown (session 41, P71-3):** all 157 history reports are Markdown files in the vault, `courses/greece/` and `courses/rome/`, read through `vault.py` and rendered by `mdcourse.history_html`; the kit's HTML fragments are retired. Every page renders the same text and figures as before. A history writer writes Markdown to the vault (`AGENT-SPEC.md`).
- **Editions and the ebook (session 38, P70, P71):** `build.py --edition greece` builds a Greece-only Academy in `editions/greece/`, `verify.py --edition greece` checks it, and `build.py --epub greece` writes an EPUB of it; neither is published. Every new course is a markdown course.
- **Reading layout (session 36, P68):** every unit and report opens on its one-line scope and reads one section at a time under a sticky bar ("Section n of N", the section menu, a progress bar, "Whole unit"); each h2 is a numbered band; Sources, the writer's opening note (a markdown unit's text before its first heading, session 37) and "How this unit was made" are closed accordions after the last section. The section last read is kept, so Continue resumes there. Theme version 6.1.
- **Figures (sessions 25 and 27):** fifteen kinds in `viz.py`: the seven for Information Architecture added by V1, four data kinds (line, bars, scatter, range) and a numeric matrix added by P44; the Figure kinds page shows one of each, sixteen figures. On a phone every figure keeps its natural size and scrolls sideways in its own frame, with a fade and a cue line. Figures added after writing (sessions 29, 30 and 32): 38 in the Brand + AI units, 180 in the Rome reports (177, then 3 written back by P57), 260 in the Greek reports, each checked by a separate Opus 5.5 agent; a Fable 5.1 check is owed (P48). Since session 31 (P55) the renderer moves or widens a label that cannot fit instead of clipping it, stops the build on what it cannot draw honestly (a year 0, a list given as a string, years BC without `years`), and `verify.py` checks the geometry of every figure on every page. Since session 37 (H-2) it draws a copy of each block and checks every block's keys, a timeline date may name a century, matrices size their columns to their contents, centred drawings open on the drawing on a phone, and object-map labels keep clear of crossings.
- **User Experience (sessions 24, 34 and 35):** a fourth track and a second markdown family, `generator/data/ux/`; its first course, IA · Information Architecture, has its frame, syllabus and briefs, IA1 (session 34, six figures) and IA2 (session 35, eight figures, the course notation every later figure uses) filed in `courses/ux/`; the course reads "in progress", 2 of 15. Its fresh checks run on Opus 5.5 until Pedro has Fable again (Fable 5.1 checks owed, row P49).

- **Rome, course 2:** complete, 79 of 79 reports, about 264,000 words, all written with Opus 5.5 and all checked by a fresh agent. The last 20 (11.3 to 11.6 in session 39; 11.7, 11.8, 12.1 to 12.8 and 13.3 to 13.8 in session 40) are flagged for the Fable review, due and deferred to stage 5.

Rome 14.3 and the Greek 13.3 high-signal pairs are done; every pair passed.

The only published page is the site; the standalone syllabus artifacts and the stray draft pages are deleted.

- **P20 (session 20):** the two syllabi and the Brand + AI briefs and bibliography pages are fetched files like the reports (`academy/<course>/syllabus.html`, `briefs.html`, `bibliography.html`); the shell is 366 KB, down from 747 KB. Site version 58.

- **DD-B3 (session 17):** course B is 8 core units and 3 deep dives; Academy shows B 2 of 11. Site version 49.

The phone layout is unchanged. `verify.py` runs a phone, tablet and desktop pass. Publishing Academy means the artifact, then `./deploy.sh`.

Row F-PB ran in two parts. Part 1, 14 Nov 2026 (Pedro away): twelve fresh Fable agents, one per unit, and one for Audit 4, every auditor stating claude-fable-5-2 (Fable 5.2, accepted by Pedro for this row); the chat ran as Fable 5.2 until 03:28, then as Opus 5.5 for saving, applying and recording, and stopped at a shorter-window Fable limit at 03:27, after 18% of the weekly Fable allowance. Part 2, 15 Nov 2026 (session https://claude.ai/code/session_01KMmgQyNZfTSHF35S2ivXpn): the chat on Opus 5.5 throughout, reading only six-line replies and stats files; four fresh Fable agents (claude-fable-5-2) re-ran PB9, PB10, DD-PB1 and DD-PB2 from their recorded samples with search, each with a full attribution pass and 40 searches at most (134 used, none refused).

- **Audits.** Audit 1 (PB1 to PB4), Audit 2 (PB5 to PB8), Audit 3 (PB9, PB10, DD-PB1, DD-PB2): every unit PASS; 155 claims sampled, 712 checked in all, 32 FAIL (5 in the samples), none STALE, 13 unverifiable; no unit reached the one-in-ten rule. Audit 4, the full read: the course delivers its goal; 19 findings, 10 contradictions between units, seam work; no REWRITE. Reports: `audits/personal-brand/audit-1.md` to `audit-4.md`, built by script from `audits/personal-brand/f-pb/`.

- **Assessment.** `audits/fable-stage-assessment-2026-11.md`, final: 2.83M tokens for the twelve unit audits, 3.64M for every agent measured; numbers fail at 1.1 per 100 after the Opus fresh checks, attribution at 8.8 (6 per 100 in the full attribution passes); recommendation: sample audits with a full attribution pass in the same agent, course B first, at most six auditors a turn at 30 searches each.

- **Build.** `build.py`, `verify.py` (ALL CHECKS PASSED). Academy artifact v157 (1794739051-70be; 18 files in one patch over v156, read back and matched). GitHub Pages deployed (01f72dc..73ffe9f; P110 closed).

## 10 November 2026 · Session 69 · User Experience DD-IA3 (U-15)

U-15 ran in one chat from about 21:53 on 10 November. The row names Opus 5.5 for the chat, and the chat ran as Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5): it only filed, built, verified, applied findings and recorded; the writing was done by a fresh Opus 5.5 agent context and the fresh check by a fresh agent on model fable (P31). The first attach of the repo was refused by the session's auto-mode check ("Access Added As A Means") and an unauthenticated clone failed; Pedro approved the attach at 21:54, and the repo was attached and cloned once (at 1c6153f); the session offered `register_repo_root` and it was called; the repo has no CLAUDE.md. The permission check did not refuse the repo's scripts. Baseline `build.py` and `verify.py`: ALL CHECKS PASSED; the baseline build (the shell carrying U-14's recorded identifiers) was committed as e4169d8. The brief was built by script with `kit/mdcourse.py`'s `open_brief` from `hub.COURSES`, no placeholder left; it occurs once, verbatim, in the prompt file the writer's prompt was pasted from, and stands byte for byte as `brief.txt` in the writer's packet. Every unit the brief's Takes line names (IA4, IA9, IA10) was filed; the pointers to DD-IA3 in IA2, IA4, IA5, IA9, IA10, IA11, IA12, DD-IA1, DD-IA2 and the register were grepped and given to the writer, section by section.

- **No transcripts.** As at U-14, this session wrote no agent transcript to disk (no `.jsonl` under `/root/.claude/` or `/tmp/`), so none of the row's scripted transcript checks could be run: not the tool names, not each fetched URL against an earlier search, not the paths, not the "model" field, and not the check that the prompt received equals the prompt sent (the prompts were pasted from files that were checked by script). What stands in their place: each agent's own statement of its model and of the tools it used (both said they saw a memory snapshot and the Project's instructions and used neither, and called no memory, Projects, Artifact, connector, Agent or `mcp__` tool); the writer's `out/log.md` (37 numbered searches and fetches, each fetch listed after a search) and `out/notes.md`; the checker's `out/log.md` (51 entries, 23 searches and 28 fetches); the writer's long hand-back, which the harness saved whole under `/tmp/claude-tool-results/` and which was copied from there by script.
- **The writer.** One fresh Opus 5.5 agent (claude-opus-5-5, as it states) on a packet folder holding exactly the brief, `write-unit`, sourcing Parts 1 and 4, the figure kinds, the Perqo register, IA1 to IA12, DD-IA1 and DD-IA2, `kit/viz.py` with `design/theme.css` beside it and a `render.py`. About 16,900 words outside its six figures; the deliverable in three `## Deliverable` sections. It took 27 minutes. Its hand-back carried the whole unit between the two marker lines; it differed from its `out/` file by one trailing newline, and the unit was filed from the `out/` file (sha256 bb9c3d21…). It reported one fetch refused (the Z39.19 PDF, by robots) and one page with no body (a Content Marketing Institute article), each recorded as not checked.
- **The fresh check.** One fresh agent on model fable, which the session gives as Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2, as it states), so DD-IA3's Fable 5.1 fresh check is owed and P49 says so. It saw only its own folder (the unit as a file with a read-only copy and the sha256 of both, confirmed at its start and its end; the sourcing standard; `units_data.py` and `hub.py`; the register; IA1 to IA12, DD-IA1 and DD-IA2; the figure kinds), with `skills/audit-units` §3b and its figures paragraph pasted verbatim, braces filled, checked by script. It took 13 minutes. Verdict fix: 3 errors (one cause in three places), 8 should-fix, 5 minor. Its report, the file it wrote to its own folder, was saved by script at `audits/ux/fresh-check-DD-IA3.md` (9dd9c1c) before any fix.
- **Findings.** Each verified against the unit, the filed units (IA2, IA4, IA5, IA8, IA9, IA10, IA12) and, for the outside sources, by the maintainer's own search: Shopify's README says its mappings join Shopify's taxonomy to other taxonomies, and the repository's integrations README, which may document mappings between Shopify's own releases, came back only through a mirror and was not fetched, so the claim of version mappings left the unit in three places; GOV.UK's principles show "Published 13 June 2019" and no update line; the W3C page has no "ratio of 3"; GOV.UK's sentence has its comma. 15 exact replacements applied by script from the checker's `fixes.json`; one minor needs no change and is recorded on the report file. The checker marked two brief items thin with no replacement: Welchman's governance models, which the maintainer fixed in part (the core and distributed teams named in Welchman's own words from her UX Booth interview, found by search and fetched once, undated on the page; chapters 2 and 6 still unread, and the unit says so), and Hedden, cited for scope only, which no page the web rule allows can fix and Audit 4 reads.
- **Figures.** Six, all passed the fresh check; no fix changed a declaration or a drawn label, so no re-check was owed. The frames of every figure at phone 320 to 430, tablet and desktop, dark and light, were looked at: each draws whole across its frames; the two-a-row flow scrolls a few pixels at 320 and 360 px, as that kind does by construction.
- **Filed units.** None changed: DD-IA3's decision log entries DL-023 to DL-026 (FO-10, the language and market review; the vocabulary's steward and RV-13 to RV-16; the change-request process across languages, the URL form in other languages and T-14's synonyms per language; R11 and R12 for IA4) are proposed, and every pointer to DD-IA3 in IA2, IA4, IA5, IA9, IA10, IA11, IA12, DD-IA1 and DD-IA2 reads true against what it built (IA10's "none yet (DD-IA3)" stays true while FO-10 is proposed). ## 8 to 9 November 2026 · Session 61 · The object reliefs rolled out to all five courses, and filed with every new unit and course (P96)

Greece 33 of 78 pages, 21 outlines in use; Rome 33 of 79, 18; Personal brand 12 of 12 (its research page included), 10; Building with AI 10 of 11 (DD-B3 none), 10; Information architecture 7 of 7, 7 (seven pages written, so seven is every page; it reaches ten as its units are filed). Every ancient outline serves both history courses where their texts name it (`p95_library.py`, after the fourth history check). The terminal is now the sign for Claude Code, the clock for scheduled tasks, the banknote's keywords add monetisation and revenue; the wooden horse serves Rome, the magnifying glass Building with AI. 77 outlines (Greece 34, Rome 35, Personal brand 16, Building with AI 19, Information architecture 16; some serve several), 52 in use, 25 marked spare in `outlines.json` (`spare`, with the reason; `p95_library.py`'s `SPARE`); 49 candidates rejected with their reasons, 27 of them in this chat (busy: Pegasus, Medusa's head, the philosopher's bust, the caduceus, the harp, a papyrus, the thunderbolt, a sheep, a charging bull, a Viking shield, a sacrificial dagger, a speedometer; not the object: lorc/bull and two tablets that read as rock, the Cyclops as an eye, an arena, a modern lighthouse, a wine glass, a cow, a tablet computer, a planked round shield, two card icons, a chart, a gauge and a speedometer whose cut-out lines read as a maze, a calculator no text names). The contact sheet, dark and light: `kit/reviews/p96/contact-sheet-*.png`.

Reports verbatim in `kit/reviews/p96/` with the change notes after each. History (Greece and Rome): FIX (4 errors, 14 should-fix, 7 minor: a laurel tree shown as a wreath, an olive bed, two helmets in passing, weak fits, a tablet that read as rock, no shield, headings as deciding sentences; 12 pages to none, a wax tablet, a bough, new sentences), FIX (2 should-fix, 8 minor: Greece 2.2's lyre and 5.3's helmet in passing; both none, better sentences, stale notes), FIX (2 should-fix, 3 minor: Greece 5.3's coinage section, outlines not matched across the two courses; 5.3 the coins, Rome 1.2 the vase, 1.6, 7.1 and 9.2 the tablet, Greece 2.4 the eagle), FIX (3 should-fix, 2 minor: the courses column still incomplete, Rome 1.1's Iguvine Tables, Greece 7.1 thin; every ancient outline matched against both courses and every newly named page decided, Greece 10.5 the sword, Rome 1.1 the tablet, 7.1 none), PASS (2 minor: reasons for none recorded for the rest, 10.5 accepted). "The courses an outline serves" reads as the courses whose texts name it, for the ancient outlines across both history courses; the everyday ones keep the courses they were taken for. ## 8 November 2026 · Session 60 · Object reliefs: the outline library, the kind `relief` and three pilot pages (P95)

P95 ran in one chat on Opus 5.5 from 19:33 on 8 November, on Pedro's decisions of 5 November (09:12: "we can even fetch svg outlines of things (the coliseum in rome, a vase for greece, stuff like that) that make sense in articles and then use the topographical method and animation to illustrate the articles"; 09:20, on the plan's part B, https://claude.ai/artifact/VxRD5FyovizffVQMxTjESZ: "I don't need a colosseum, i was just listing examples of things, you will then decide which figures make sense. And the illustration set can and should grow as we add more courses, it's ok to start small. But let's shoot for 10 per course at least. Agree on detail, follow your plan"). The clone was at 8812591; the permission check did not refuse the repo's scripts. Baseline `build.py` and `verify.py`: ALL CHECKS PASSED; the build changed only the README size table and the shell (the last record lines), committed as e21b212.

- **The library (step 2).** `design/outlines/`: one SVG per object, taken by `kit/p95_library.py` from partial clones (`--depth 1 --filter=blob:none --no-checkout`, then `git show` of only the files needed) of game-icons/icons (82d9488, CC BY 3.0), phosphor-icons/core (2b75f3a, MIT) and tabler/tabler-icons (58f6053, MIT); unchanged but for the background square game-icons adds, removed by script; filled silhouettes only. `outlines.json` records for each its id, file, what it depicts (a sign said to be one: "an envelope (the sign for email)"), the courses it serves, its keywords and the homonyms taken out (`excludes`: each takes out the whole sentence that holds it), its repository, path, commit and URL, author, licence and its link, the credit line, and for each course the texts that name it with their counts, read from the prose (front matter, figure declarations, link targets and URLs taken out first). `licences/` holds each set's licence file at the commit taken. 48 outlines: Greece 12 (amphora, temple front, Ionic column, oared warship, lyre, owl, olive, laurel, theatre masks, the wooden horse, centaur, Minotaur), Rome 11 (amphora, temple front, warship, laurel, crested Roman helmet, short sword, toga, eagle, wolf's head, stone bridge, torch), Personal brand 10 (karate gi, video camera, microphone, phone, calendar, receipt, envelope, folder, picture, banknote), Building with AI 12 (picture, calendar, envelope, folder, terminal window, plug, servers, clock, open book, checklist, database, laptop), Information architecture 15 (phone, tag, receipt, envelope, checklist, folded map, sitemap, tree diagram, books, bicycle, barbell, fork and knife, payment card, bell, magnifying glass); 20 from game-icons (Delapouite, Lorc, Skoll), 27 from Phosphor, 1 from Tabler. 22 candidates are listed under "rejected" with their reasons: busy (aqueduct, Roman shield, sundial, ruins, Spartan helmet, sphinx, chariot, barn owl: thin parts; coins: 45 regions; labyrinth, broken columns, black belt: a step under 3 px) or not the object the texts name (a vase on a capital, a later galley, a modern arch bridge, a crowned wreath, a wax tablet no Roman text names, a flask for "AI labs", a robot for "robots.txt", a brain for "brain dump", a second camera, a bar chart named only in passing).
- **The tool and the kind (step 3).** `kit/relief.py`, run when an outline is added (not by the build): renders each SVG in Chromium at 512 px, takes its signed distance field (`distance_transform_edt` inside and out) and contours it (`find_contours`): the outline and up to seven levels inside at a ninth of the deepest distance, two halo levels outside; simplifies with `viz.py`'s RDP, normalises to a 1000-unit box, writes `<id>.json`; it measures and refuses a busy relief (more than 40 regions; a part under 3% of its width, read as one that runs at least 8% of the width, so a corner the measuring disc rounds off is not a part; a step under 3 px, added after the first check, where thin strokes pack their levels into solid bands). `viz.py`'s home kind `relief` (in `HOME_KINDS`, not on the Figure kinds page) draws `div.topo.relief`, aria-hidden and textless, one `svg.topo-l` in the tone's class with `path.rl-out` (the halo) and `path.rl-in`, its frame grown so every line stays 14 units clear at both ends of the turn at the top of the breath (`rel_frame`, the turn and breath read from the theme), simplified further until at most 12 KB as the page holds it; `--ph` and `--ar` the only style. `mdcourse.reading` puts it, as `figure.relief-fig` with its caption, under the scope line and before the section bar, from `design/outlines/uses.json` (panel id to outline), at a phase halfway between the page's first two separators'; the caption: "<what it shows>, a stylised outline drawn as a relief from an icon. <credit>." (the adaptation said, as CC BY 3.0 asks), its licence linked (the CC BY 3.0 deed; an MIT set's LICENSE at the commit). Each course's How it was built page (`howbuilt.credits_section`) lists its pages' reliefs under "Illustration credits", with each one's source and commit, the library's size for the course, and each set's licence file in full (MIT asks its notice to go with every copy). Theme 6.8 (section 9): `.relief-fig`, `.topo.relief` (the frame at `--ar`, the column's width and `--relief-h` tall at most: 280 px on a phone, 340 from 768 px), `--topo-r` (the layer, .85 dark, .8 light) and `--topo-rh` (the halo within it, .4 and .45), the caption sharing `.viz figcaption`'s rule. The sourcing standard's Part 1 gains the illustration rule.
- **The pilot (step 4).** Greece 4.3, "The fall of Troy: the lost Epic Cycle": the wooden horse (its section 6 is "The Wooden Horse: Laocoön, Sinon, Cassandra"; "Epeius, by Athena's design, builds the Wooden Horse."). Rome 1.4, "Romulus and Remus": a wolf's head ("A she-wolf coming down to drink heard the crying, suckled them and licked them"; section 7 on the wolf-and-twins objects). Personal brand A5, "Production craft": a phone (section 1, "One phone, one tripod, one lav": "The phone on a tripod. One static camera set up before the warm-up and left alone."); the first choice there, a microphone, was a stand microphone where the unit's is a clip-on lavalier, and was changed on the first pages' check. The reliefs weigh 5,916, 6,054 and 1,357 B as the pages hold them.
- **Checks (step 5).** `verify.py`'s `check_reliefs`: the library (at least ten outlines per course, every field, an allowed licence named in its credit, its SVG and JSON, a text of each course naming it); every reading page has a relief exactly where `uses.json` maps one; each mapped page at 320, 390, 768 and 1280 px, dark and light, with and without motion: its place, its caption and credit exactly with the licence linked, classes only, aria-hidden and textless, at most 12 KB, the page's tone, the halo fainter, its frame, a turn and a breath by transform only at a phase no separator has, every line inside the frame at five poses at the top of the breath, still under reduced motion, the page's text naming the object, no sideways scroll. The contact sheet of every outline as its relief, dark and light: `reviews/p95/contact-sheet-dark.png` and `-light.png` (`p95_sheet.py`); the pilot shots at six widths and both themes and the five poses: `reviews/p95/shots/` (`p95_shots.py`).
- **The checkers (step 6).** Fresh Opus 5.5 agents (claude-opus-5-5 in every transcript), each given only its folder (`p95_extract.py`), their transcripts checked by `p95_transcripts.py` (the model, Read, Glob and Grep only, every path inside the folder matched with and without its trailing slash, the report taken from its hand-back). Reports verbatim in `reviews/p95/`. A, the library: FIX (22 paths; errors: a flask, a robot and a black belt no text names as such; should-fix: the MIT notice, a camera counted twice, the brain, signs captioned as things, homonyms in the Ionic column, the masks, the wax tablet, the terminal; busy reliefs; all fixed); FIX (22 paths; masks, a video camera and a payment card captioned as things, timber bridges, revenue receipts, front matter and URLs counted; fixed, and the bar chart removed); FIX (17 paths; excludes that took a name but left its sentence, masks and bridges; fixed by excluding the whole sentence); FIX (21 paths; should-fix: "centurion" counted a rank, not the helmet; minors: a decimal point cut a sentence, a unit's source titles counted, map as a verb, olive; all fixed: the helmet counted by helmets only, decimals and abbreviations end no sentence, a unit's Sources section not counted); PASS (17 paths; minors: Cicero's *In toga candida* and "MCP server" counted, fixed; the Ionic column's two texts, settled: both name the order the drawing shows). B, the pilot pages: FIX (58 paths; should-fix: A5's stand microphone against the unit's lavalier; fixed by showing the phone); PASS (58 paths; minors taken: "phone line" and "from a phone" out of the counts, the caption saying the relief is drawn from an icon); PASS (59 paths; minors: "icon. Icon:" reads twice, settled because the row prints the credit line exactly; uses.json's example fixed). Settled with their reasons: the wolf snarls where the text's wolf suckles (the text never says how it looked, the caption claims a wolf's head, the sets hold no calmer wolf: lorc/wolf-howl is busy, lorc/direwolf snarls too); the wolf is dense at 1x (its lines stay separate, one rule for every outline, clear on a phone); the phone is upright (the text never describes it); the crested helmets of the Villanovans and Etruscans count for the crested Roman helmet; the owl on Athens' coins counts for the owl; bibliography titles still counted where no outline's place rests on them.
- **Results.** `verify.py` on the published build: ALL CHECKS PASSED (its relief lines: the library holds 48 outlines, Greece 12, Rome 11, Personal brand 10, Building with AI 12, Information architecture 15, 22 rejected, licences CC BY 3.0 and MIT; 3 mapped pages, the wooden horse 5,916 B, the wolf 6,054 B, the phone 1,357 B, 184 reading pages without one; frames 272 x 280, 300 x 280 and 202 x 280 px at 390, 331 x 340, 364 x 340 and 245 x 340 from 768; the closest line 3.2 px from its frame over the poses with motion; the separators unchanged from P94, 1,591 on 187 pages); `verify.py --edition greece`: ALL CHECKS PASSED. The contact sheet: `reviews/p95/contact-sheet-dark.png` and `-light.png`; the pilot shots: `reviews/p95/shots/`.
- **Choices taken.** The library's outlines are chosen for literal objects the texts discuss, and where a keyword names a thing by its sign the caption says so ("(the sign for email)"). Phosphor's filled icons serve the three courses of everyday objects, game-icons the ancient ones. An outline may serve several courses. The thin-part rule is read as a part, not a corner (8% run); the step rule is this chat's working of "busy". The frame is the drawing's aspect, left-aligned in the column like the text. 280 px on a phone, 340 from 768 px (the row sets only the phone). The caption adds "drawn as a relief from an icon" to the row's form, because CC BY 3.0 asks an adaptation to say so. Theme tokens: the relief reads stronger than a separator (.85 against .62), being the page's one illustration.

## 8 November 2026 · Session 59 · Section separators: every section of every reading page ends on a topography of its paragraphs (P94)

P94 ran in one chat on Opus 5.5 from 11:28 on 8 November, on Pedro's decisions of 5 November (09:12: "These topographical abstract illustrations are great. We could use them as paragraph separators (the ones that are more horizontal)"; 09:20, on the plan's part A, https://claude.ai/artifact/VxRD5FyovizffVQMxTjESZ: "follow your plan", "implement this asap before finishing the UX course"). The clone was at 631dcd3; the permission check did not refuse the repo's scripts. Baseline `build.py` and `verify.py`: ALL CHECKS PASSED; the build changed the README size table and the shell's Changelog and Backlog pages (the rows committed after the last build), committed as 0a8d121. The shots of the start are `reviews/p94/before/` (Greece 6.1, Rome 1.1 and IA1 at 390 and 1280 px, dark and light: the top of each page and the end of section 1), by `p94_shots.py`.

- **The separators (step 2).** `mdcourse.reading`, which lays out every unit and report of both families, ends each section that has a top-level paragraph on a separator, before its next and previous: `div.topo.topo-patch.topo-sep`, aria-hidden and textless, drawn by `build.py`'s `sep_block` (handed to `reading` by `sep_kit`, from `load` for a history report and from `mdcourse.build` for a markdown unit) with `viz.py`'s `contours`: one rise per top-level `<p>` of the section's own text (`mdcourse.top_paras`: the `<p>` children of the section, the text before a report's first heading belonging to section 1, its words counted as a report's are; lists, tables, figures, quotes, boxes, the band and the next and previous are not paragraphs; an empty one is left out), in order along a ridge (`ct_ridge`, in a box from .1 to .9 of the width and .42 to .58 of the height, so the ridge is nearly level), a contour every 5.5th of the section's largest paragraph, in the course's tone (a history report its course's colour, as its home's hero); 1200 by 170 units fitted whole in its frame (`meet`). Each hill is drawn out along the ridge (the layout's new `stretch`, from about the distance to the next rise, between 2.5 and 6 times its height) so neighbours meet in one ridge; its height across the ridge starts at 24 units, less where the ridge is dense; the fit (`turned_clear`, `turned_inside` split by direction) lowers the hills where a pose takes a line past the top or bottom and draws them out less where it takes one past an end, until every rise's rings and every line stay at least 6 units clear of the frame at both ends of the turn, at rest and at the top of the breath; then the lines are simplified further until the separator is at most 6 KB as the page holds it (`sep_bytes`: the HTML parser writes a self-closed SVG element with an end tag, which the first count missed). No rise is ever dropped. Each rise is labelled by its place (¶1, ¶2 ...) and the section's id seeds the outlines (`seed`), so every separator's shapes are its own; its rises carry no `data-at` or `data-box` (only a hero's checks read them). Each separator of a page starts at its own point of the move (`phases` over the page's separators). 1,591 separators on the 187 reading pages; 330 sections (a quiz, a carry-forward list, a table or a list alone) have no paragraph and so none. 2,303,391 bytes in the build (2,349,505 as the pages hold them), 23,797 on the heaviest page (IA6, eleven separators); the largest separator 6,118 B as the page holds it; the site 12.86 to 15.07 MB; the shell 830 to 840 KB (the theme's new rules and this record). With the separators stripped, every page was byte-identical to the start build (checked before the renderer fixes below, which then changed the text of nine units' pages only as recorded there).
- **Faster contours.** `viz.py`'s field samples each rise only inside the window where its bump is above zero and sums each node in the same order, so the field is identical node for node (every home, course home and What's new byte for byte the same) and the 1,591 separators build in about 40 s: the build takes about 75 s, from 28.
- **Theme 6.7 (step 2).** Section 9: `.topo.topo-patch.topo-sep{aspect-ratio:1200 / 170;margin:30px 0 0}` (the column's width; 30 px under the text, and the next and previous keep their own 30 px above), its layer at the new token `--topo-s` (.62 dark, .6 light, the patch's value, so a separator reads as quietly as a tile's patch; its own token so it can be tuned alone). In "Whole unit" the next and previous are hidden and the separator stands between its section and the next, 34 px above the next band. It turns and breathes as every contour drawing (4i). Under 360 px the next and previous label is 10 px without tracking and the button's sides 10 px (a two-digit "Previous · 10 of 11" ran past its button; found by the first checker).
- **Checks (step 3).** `verify.py`'s `check_seps`, on every reading page at 320, 390, 768 and 1280 px, dark and light, under reduced motion and with motion allowed (2,992 page states): every section with a top-level paragraph ends on one separator, last before its next and previous, a section without one has none; its rises against the paragraphs read from the page (count and words; the cue `shell.js` puts under a wide figure is the figure's, not a paragraph); class, aria-hidden, textless, at most 6 KB as the page holds it, its lines in the page's tone (the panel's `--cc`), its frame 1200:170 and the column's width, 20 px clear at least of the text above and the next and previous below and, in "Whole unit", of the next section; every next and previous label inside its button; with motion a turn and a breath on each, transform only, distinct phases, and at five poses of the turn at the top of the breath every line inside its frame; nothing moving under reduced motion; no page wider than the screen. Tried against a breath of 1.8 (it failed every separator) and the old label style (it failed every two-digit label). `check_topo` leaves the separators to it (a reading page's topography is no longer "a topography on a page that has none"). Every existing reading check passes.
- **Found by the checker and fixed.** Sections of many paragraphs drew round hills and dots in narrow slots, not a horizontal ridge (B7 §10, 38 rises; IA2 §1, 25): every hill is now at least 2.5 times as wide as it is tall, lowered where the ridge is dense. A separator drops closed rings under 6 units both ways (zero-area slivers, `SEP_SPECK`), and a rise under one level gets its spot ring only where it has no ring of its own (separators only; the tiles and heroes are unchanged). Fenced code blocks written at the top level of three units rendered as paragraphs with their fences showing (`render_md` lacked `fenced_code`): Personal brand A1's bio drafts, Building with AI B7's two thumbnail styles (its "> " readouts had become a quotation) and DD-B1's skill file (its "---" line had become a heading, a section of its own named "```"). They now render as code. Found by the second checker: a list written straight under a line of text, with no blank line between, rendered inside that paragraph, its "- " and "1. " marks showing and its words counted in the paragraph's rise (Personal brand A1, A2, A4, A9, DD-A1; Building with AI B5, DD-B1, DD-B3; IA1; and, in the Sources accordion, five history bibliographies); `mdcourse.list_breaks` puts a blank line before the first item of such a list (never inside a fenced block, an open list, a table or a quotation) in every Markdown renderer (`render_md`, `history_html`, the bibliographies in `build.py`): 63 lists, each checked by eye in the source. A separator's rise that closes no ring of its own (under one level, or its level rings merged into its neighbours' lines: eight rises in the sample drew nothing) gets its spot ring (`_ct_spotted`), and a closed ring under 2 units across either way, a flat dash, is dropped (`SEP_FLAT`); `check_seps` fails a rise that draws nothing. Found by the third checker: rings 3 to 5 units tall still drew as one solid dash at 390 px (0.3 px a unit, two strokes merging), so `SEP_FLAT` is 8 units (2.4 px at 390 px), tested on each whole contour line before it is shared out among the rises; a rise drawn by its spot ring alone carries no empty path. The source text of every unit is unchanged; the counts the pages show follow the corrected rendering: A1 5,280 to 5,269 words, A2 5,621 to 5,604, A4 6,024 to 6,006, A9 6,678 to 6,650, DD-A1 7,365 to 7,334, B5 8,648 to 8,642, B7 7,482 to 7,483 (its "> " readouts now counted as code text), DD-B1 7,435 to 7,422 and 10 to 9 sections, DD-B3 8,948 to 8,792, IA1 5,412 to 5,399. No course's hours of reading change (16.4, 17.6, 4.8, 5.3, 4.4), and no history report's words.
- **The checkers (step 4).** Fresh Opus 5.5 agents (claude-opus-5-5 in every transcript), each given only its folder (the extract of every separator on the three shot pages and twenty more chosen by script, `p94_extract.py`, seeded, the section with most paragraphs and the heaviest page put in; the shots and the cuts of each separator at 320, 390, 768 and 1280 px, dark and light, `p94_shots.py`; the theme's section 9; a README with the row's words); their transcripts checked by script: only Read, Glob and Grep, every path inside the folder. Reports verbatim in `reviews/p94/`. First: FIX (80 calls, 79 paths, none outside its folder; should-fix: the dense ridges, the label at 320 px, the fenced blocks; minors: spot rings beside rings, zero-area slivers, the faint IA blue in the dark theme, a section of short paragraphs as dots, the choice note's bytes, the pose count's wording; all fixed but two settled: the IA separators are as faint as every IA tile's patch, the same tone at the same opacity, decorative and aria-hidden; a section of mostly short paragraphs reads as a few dots and a hill because its paragraphs are short, at the row's interval). Second: FIX (94 calls; an error: lists written under a line counted as paragraphs; should-fix: rises that drew nothing, flat rings that blocked a spot ring; minors: a stale paragraph count in its README, the IA blue), all fixed. Third: FIX (102 calls; should-fix: rings 3 to 5 units tall drew as solid dashes at 390 px; minors: large paragraphs marked only by their spot ring inside a merged ridge, empty paths, `data-rings` naming levels), the dashes and empty paths fixed; settled: the spot rings (the ridge's lines go to whichever rise dominates each stretch), `data-rings` (its meaning since P93, read by the other checks). Fourth: FIX (89 calls; its should-fix: the renderer fixes changed displayed stats against the row; settled with the reason under Choices; minors: label-like paragraphs, a spot ring inside a neighbour's hill, specks), the spot rings made at least 5 units in radius. Fifth: PASS (123 calls; minors settled: two small rings, 19 by 9 and 48 by 10 units, read as short dashes at 320 px, but raising the cut-off further would drop real level rings of short paragraphs; large paragraphs marked by spot rings as above; B7 §10's separator 26 B under the cap, which `sep_block` keeps by simplifying further, so a later edit cannot break it). Every transcript: claude-opus-5-5 only, Read, Glob and Grep only, every path inside its folder (79, 93, 101, 88 and 122 paths).
- **Results.** `verify.py` on the published build: ALL CHECKS PASSED (its separator line: 187 reading pages, 1,921 sections, 1,591 separators, 330 without, 2,349,505 bytes as the pages hold them, the largest 6,118 B, the closest line 1.3 px from its frame over 63,640 poses with motion); `verify.py --edition greece`: ALL CHECKS PASSED (78 pages, 661 separators, 165 sections without). The after shots: `reviews/p94/after/` (the three pages' tops, the end of section 1 and "Whole unit", at 390 and 1280 px, dark and light; every separator cut at 320, 390, 768 and 1280 px in `after/cuts/`).
- **Choices taken.** The separator is the column's width (the paragraphs' measure), not the band's full bleed. The ridge is nearly level (its swing a tenth of what What's new's is), so the drawing reads horizontal at every width. The hills are drawn out along the ridge (the plan's "about 7:1" frame filled by a ridge, not a row of islands), between 2.5 and 6 times their height. A section's paragraphs are the `<p>` children of its own text; the text a report opens with before its first heading is section 1's. An empty paragraph is not one. The 6 KB is measured as the page holds the element, the same way the heroes' 20 KB is. The rise labels are their places (¶n), the section seeding the shapes. Lines at the patch's opacity, under a token of their own. The renderer fixes (fenced code, lists under a line), older faults than this row, were an error and a should-fix of its checks; the row asks for every finding to be fixed in the session rather than proposed (Pedro, 2 Nov 2026: "Don't want more proposed tasks. Resolve everything in this session"), so they were made here, and the counts they change are recorded above. The row's "the words of a section, a unit's stats and a course's hours do not change" is read as a rule on the separators, which change none (checked by stripping them and comparing every page); no unit's source text changed, and no course's hours. The fourth checker returned FIX on that reading alone (its should-fix: the stats changed against the row), and it is settled here with this reason; its minors: label-like paragraphs ("Body", "Before every session:") are top-level paragraphs by the row's rule and stay; a small paragraph's spot ring inside its neighbour's hill follows the data; the smallest spot rings drew as specks, so a separator's spot ring is now at least 5 units in radius (`SEP_SPOT`, 3 px across at 390 px); the faint IA blue as before. Separators also print (the row does not mention print).

## 4 November 2026 · Session 58 · Every contour drawing in slow motion, topographies on the course homes and What's new, the tile across the site (P93)

P93 ran in one chat on Opus 5.5 from 15:55 on 4 November, on Pedro's decision of 15:43, made on P92's home ("Can we animate that topographical illustration, very slow, expanding and rotating movements, across the board, and expand that to other pages, and make this new tile style propagate across the site as well as this new topo illustration? It looks freaking awesome!!!"). The clone was at 0cb6dd5; the permission check did not refuse the repo's scripts. Baseline `build.py` and `verify.py`: ALL CHECKS PASSED; the build changed the README size table and the shell's Backlog page (P93's queued row), committed as 77a902a. The shots of the start are `reviews/p93/before/` (the home, What's new and the five course homes at 320, 360, 390, 430, 768 and 1280 px, dark and light, device scale 2, from a copy of the baseline so the work could go on; Rome at 320, 360 and 430 px with verify's own page setup, since `--shots` took the second history course only at three widths). No unit and no history report's text changed. Greece's course home changed, so the Greece edition was built, verified and republished (below).

- **The move (step 2).** P92's 8 x 6 px drift (`topo-drift`) is gone. Every contour drawing, the home's hero, every tile's patch and every new topography, makes one slow move where motion is welcome: `topo-turn`, a turn from -3 to +3 degrees and a growth from 1 to 1.06 about its centre over 120 s each way, ease-in-out, alternating, transform only (theme tokens `--topo-turn`, `--topo-grow`, `--topo-cycle`); each drawing of a page starts at its own point of the cycle (`--ph`, from `build.py`'s `phases`: k/n of the way in a stride order, so neighbours differ by more than 1/n); under reduced motion nothing moves on any page. `.topo` is a size container, so each layout's overscan is computed on its own frame, half of `100cqh * sin(turn)` each side and half of `100cqw * sin(turn)` above and below, plus a pixel; a patch's drawing fits its frame (`meet`, no overscan), its rings kept clear of the frame at every pose (`turned_inside`: each box turned and grown to both ends of the move). Every text over a topography is measured at five poses (the two ends of the sweep, its quarters and its middle; on the home with each area word in turn); `--topo-a` came down from .2 to .16 in both themes by the row's rule: at .2 a muted lede over two rings passing within a pixel measured 4.11 to 4.48:1 in the light theme.
- **The topographies (step 3).** Each course home's hero carries one (`hero_layer`): one rise per unit in its order (a history course's syllabus units, the audit unit left out, as its tile's patch), a written unit by its words, a planned unit a dashed ring, a contour every 5.5th of its largest unit; a tall layout (a square) below 1100 px and a wide one (2:1) from it, its rises on a serpentine lattice (`ct_lattice`) and sized (`ct_fit`) so each written rise closes min(2, its levels) rings of its own and stands, turned and grown to both ends of the move, inside the window a 390 px phone, the 768 px tablet and the 1280 px desktop all show, above the fade. The layer runs from the top bar to the hero's foot, the full width beside the sidebar (`.hero.has-topo`); the hero keeps its own height, because it holds the stats and the buttons, whose boxes are opaque, so the lines pass under them. What's new's hero carries one rise per day it lists, oldest at the left along a ridge (`ct_ridge`), its height the words filed that day, a contour every 11,000 words (`NEW_INTERVAL`: 22 Oct, 221,673 words, stands 20 levels; 30 Oct, 14,178, 1.29, a ring of its own), each day in the tone of the course that filed the most words that day; its layouts 1.6:1 and 3.7:1 for its flatter hero. The Changelog, the Backlog, the Figure kinds page and the reading pages have none. Layers, as verify measures them: the home 14.3 KB, Greece 8.3, Rome 9.8, Personal brand 10.9, Building with AI 10.0, Information architecture 8.3, What's new 11.0 (every one aria-hidden and textless, each at most 20 KB). The shell would have been 945 KB with the markdown course homes' tiles and layers in it, 79 KB short of the limit with eight IA units to come, so the markdown course homes are fetched files now, as the history homes are (`<course>/home.html`); the shell is 789 KB.
- **The tile (step 4).** One builder, `tile()`, for every course, unit and planned course: the chip with the status pill under it, the title, a two-line line, three stats, its own patch (`patch_block`). A history unit's tile (`unit_tile`, replacing `ucard`) is the summary of a disclosure that opens to its module rows (its stats modules written, in all, hours; its patch its modules by their words; opened, its line whole); a markdown unit's (in `mdcourse.py`) is the link to its reading page, or to its brief while not written (`.pending`, one dashed ring; its stats sections, words, hours; its patch its sections by their words); each of History's eight planned courses is a ghost (`.ghost`: the chip outlined, the title, its scope in two lines, no stats, one dashed ring, opening to its spine). Two columns from 768 px, a lone tile half the width on every page; while a unit tile is open its grid's tiles take their own heights. The unit cards' and the planned rows' classes are gone (`.ucard`, `.ucards`, `.uch`, `.uwhy`, `.ncard`, `details.course.soon`, `.soonlist`).
- **Theme 6.6 (step 5).** 4i: the move, the overscan, `.hero.has-topo`; 4h: the tile everywhere (the home-only rules now hold for every tile), its disclosure, ghost and pending forms; 6: `.cgrid` for every list of tiles; 7: the tile's slack above its stats; 4a, 4b, 4c: the eyebrow's course label and a pill's word their tone mixed 78% with `--fg` in both themes (the dark blue label measured 4.31:1 on the page), a plain pill in `--muted` (in `--dim` about 3.5:1 on a dark card), a filled button's label at full strength in both themes (black at .85 gave 4.1:1 on the dark blue), breadcrumb separators in `--muted` (3.7:1 in `--dim`). `contrast.py` measures the new pairs in both themes (`reviews/p93/contrast.txt`: all pass).
- **Found and fixed on the way.** A markdown unit's words counted its sections' next and previous buttons, their band numerals and the token `steps">` of the report's opening tag (build and verify alike); a unit's words are now its sections' own text (`mdcourse.section_text`), about 3% fewer (Personal brand's hours 5.0 to 4.8, Building with AI's 5.5 to 5.3). The Greece edition had not built since P92 (its `HERO_AT`, in the full Academy's area order, placed four rises for one area); `HERO_AT` is keyed by area. `verify.py --edition greece` could not have passed since P92 either (its home check hard-coded the full Academy's Planned list and site links); it takes what an edition omits. A rise's levels were read from the field at its nearest grid node, so a rise barely over a level lost it and one unit could count differently in two layouts; each peak stands on a grid node and a rise's levels are its own words over the interval.
- **Checks (step 6).** `verify.py` adds `check_topo` (every layer against the sources, aria-hidden, textless, at most 20 KB, one layout shown, the own rings, every rise in view at 390 px and up and above the fade, the frame covered by the turned layout, every patch's lines inside its frame, no topography on the pages that have none; with motion only transform and opacity, every drawing moving at its own phase; under reduced motion none), `check_tiles` (one class set, the fields, each patch against its sources, a history unit's rows, a markdown unit's link, the ghosts, the grids, nothing past an edge) and `texts_contrast` (every hero text over a topography against the pixels under it at five poses), at six widths, dark and light, on the home, the five course homes, What's new, the Changelog, the Backlog, the Figure kinds page and one reading page per course; LCP and the longest task at 390 px with motion allowed on the home, Greece's home and What's new; `--shots` takes the course homes and What's new at every width and the motion sheets (the layer's own frame at five poses) of the home, Greece and Information architecture. On the way its own faults were found and fixed: a pose paused the theme switch's colour transitions half-way (a transition outranks even an `!important` rule), a `color-mix()` colour computes as `color(srgb …)` in 0 to 1, a short phone's screen ended above the hero's foot, a clamped line computes as `flow-root` in this Chromium, and a hidden grid's columns compute as written (`repeat(2, minmax(0px, 1fr))`), so verify counts a grid's tracks at depth 0.
- **Results.** `verify.py` on the published build: ALL CHECKS PASSED (`reviews/p93/verify-after.txt`); `verify.py --edition greece`: ALL CHECKS PASSED; `contrast.py`: every pair passes (`reviews/p93/contrast.txt`). LCP at 390 px with motion, on a fresh load from the local server: the home 120 ms, Greece 172 ms, What's new 128 ms on the first published build, 116, 176 and 220 ms on the second; the longest task 0 ms, no task over 50 ms, on both. Shell 789 KB (807,802 bytes) on the first published build, 791 KB (810,296 bytes) on the second (its records longer), 207 fetched files The after shots and motion sheets: `reviews/p93/after/`.
- **Found by the checks and fixed.** At 320 px a chip that would not wrap ("Information architecture · DD-IA1/2/3") widened its grid's one column 11 px past the grid: a tile grid's columns are `minmax(0, 1fr)` and the chip breaks between its two parts where it does not fit; `verify.py` checks every tile against its grid. A tile's stats are three columns each its content's width, spread across the tile, so "modules written" keeps one line where it fits. `contrast.py` prints the two-lines-stacked column as a record, not a bar (the built page, at five poses, is the authority).
- **Third follow-up: the breath, not a heartbeat (5 Nov 2026, 07:49).** Pedro: "Itsm wasn't supposed to he that fast and to pop within the outer line. The whole drawing was supposed to flowingly expand and contract, slowishly but fluidly. Even the outer edges". The heartbeat is gone (`viz.py` back to one path per rise, no `.ct-b`, no per-rise centres or caps; `--topo-beat`, `--topo-pulse` removed). Every drawing now breathes as a whole: `topo-breathe` swells the layer about its centre from 1 to `--topo-grow` (1.1) and back over `--topo-breath` (6 s) each way, eased, alternating, added to the turn (`animation-composition: replace, add`), each drawing at its own phase; the turn is rotation only (it carried the growth to 1.06 until now). Every line and every gap grows with it, the outer edges furthest; transform only, on the layer, so the compositor does the work (the heartbeat's main-thread repaints are gone). The layer keeps carrying the opacity. `build.py` reads the growth from the theme, so every rise's clearance and window take the 1.1; the wide course-home layout's window starts 10% down, not 6% (at 1.1 Personal brand's A6 reached above the desktop frame by 3 px). `verify.py`: a breath per drawing as a turn per drawing, the poses at the top of the breath, the speed from both. Verified (ALL CHECKS PASSED, the Greece edition too: `reviews/p93/verify-followup3.txt`); at 390 px with motion the move peaks at 11.4 px/s at the home layer's corners; LCP 136 ms on the home, 280 ms on Greece, 184 ms on What's new, no task over 50 ms. Each artifact read first and found at this session's own version, then published: Academy v131 (1793867381-0ca8), the Greece edition v8 (1793867398-e60d), Pages apcanario/academy fe427f5, run 41994948145, success.
- **Second follow-up: faster, and the heartbeat (5 Nov 2026, 04:11; the heartbeat superseded at 07:49, below the third).** Pedro, at 20 s: "It's still too slow. Looks good but i could be faster, and it still lacks de lines expanding from the center and spreading and contracting in all animations to give a heartbeat feeling". The sweep is 10 s (about 7 px/s at most at a 390 px phone's corners). The heartbeat: every ring a written rise closes on its own is now its own path (`.ct-b`), and every contour drawing's rings, a hero's and every tile's, beat about their rise's peak (`--cx`, `--cy` on the rise's group, set by `viz.py`): out and back, a strong beat and a weaker one (lub-dub), once every 1.6 s (`--topo-beat` 1.1, `--topo-pulse` 1.6 s), all together. Each ring takes its rank's share of the beat (`--b`, 1 for the outermost), so the rings spread apart and draw back together and never cross; `viz._ct_beat_caps` caps a rise's share where its outermost ring would reach another line (the stretches two rises share, which stay still so no line parts at a hand-over, and the other rises' rings at half the distance). A rise's `data-box` and `build.py`'s clearance now take the rings at the top of the beat, about the peak. Tried and dropped on the way: a ripple (each ring .08 s after the one inside it) crossed the rings and stacked two lines under What's new's lede (4.25:1); rings beating about their own centres, which differ a little, met in a dense rise. The layer now carries the opacity (`--topo-a`, `--topo-p` on `.topo-l`), not each line, so lines that touch or cross composite as one: splitting the rings into separate paths had stacked touching lines of What's new's 22 Oct under the lede, at rest. `verify.py`: poses put every ring at the top of its beat; the beat must run on every page with motion (transform only) and nothing beats under reduced motion; own rings are counted over all of a rise's paths; the phases are read from `--ph` (at a 10 s sweep the measured progress also carried when each drawing was first shown); the speed bound is 2 to 12 px/s. `contrast.py` notes that stacked lines now composite as one. Verified (ALL CHECKS PASSED, the Greece edition too: `reviews/p93/verify-followup2.txt`); at 390 px with motion LCP 240 ms on the home, 268 ms on Greece, 244 ms on What's new, the longest task 51 and 57 ms on the home and Greece (one task each over 50 ms; none before the beat: every ring's beat repaints its drawing on the main thread, as an SVG child's transform is not composited). Each artifact read first and found at this session's own version, then published: Academy v130 (1793856418-d6a5), the Greece edition v7 (1793856457-2bbe), Pages apcanario/academy 64aa7cf, run 41974175151, success.
- **Follow-up: the move made visible (4 Nov 2026, 20:16).** Pedro: "The animations dont work, at least on mobile". They ran (a 390 px phone's home layer turned from -3.00 to -2.97 degrees in 5 s), but at a 120 s sweep a corner of the layer moved 0.6 px/s at most, slower near the text and almost still at each end of the sweep, so the move read as still. `--topo-cycle` is 20 s: the same turn and growth, about 3.8 px/s at most at the corners, 2.2 on average, still very slow. Nothing else changes (the poses and the clearance depend on the turn and growth, not the time). `verify.py` now requires the move to be seen and to stay slow: the peak speed at the corners of a 390 px phone's home layer between 2 and 8 px/s, from the tokens and the frame, and the layer's transform changed between two reads 2 s apart. A phone with Reduce Motion (iOS) or Remove animations (Android) on still sees no move, by the row's rule. Rebuilt and verified (ALL CHECKS PASSED, the move at 3.5 px/s by verify's estimate; the Greece edition too: `reviews/p93/verify-followup.txt`), each artifact read first and found at this session's own version, then published: Academy v129 (1793824489-e4dc), the Greece edition v6 (1793824510-9955), Pages apcanario/academy 99d6f9b, run 41908035663, success.

While a tile of the grid is open, its tiles' summaries take their own height (theme 6.6, 6); `verify.py` checks that no closed tile beside an open one carries slack above its stats (it fails on the old rule, at 768 and 1280 px). Neither checker nor verify had caught it. Rebuilt, verified (ALL CHECKS PASSED; the Greece edition too; LCP at 390 px with motion: the home 116 ms, Greece 176 ms, What's new 220 ms, no task over 50 ms) and published a second time, each artifact read first and found at this session's own version: Academy v128 (1793823123-c5d6) from 043e50c, the Greece edition v5 (1793823151-50e6), Pages apcanario/academy af57c5c, run 41904417910, success.
- **The checkers (step 7).** Fresh Opus 5.5 agents (claude-opus-5-5 in every transcript), each given only its folder, their reports verbatim in `reviews/p93/` (`check-motion-1.md`, `-2`; `check-tiles-1.md` to `-4`). Motion and topographies: FIX (63 calls; an error: 30 Oct stood flat in the tall layout while it held a level in the wide, its levels read from the field at the nearest grid node: each peak now stands on a node and a rise's levels are its own words over the interval), then PASS (71). Tiles: FIX (78; an error: a third line showed under the clamp in a tile stretched beside a taller one, so the slack goes above the stats; should-fix: the plain pill in `--dim`, now `--muted`; the stats wrapping, now three columns), FIX (64; should-fix: a section under one contour level drew no ring, so it takes a spot ring, `.ct-spot`, and the record carries each tile's modifiers, which verify checks), FIX (41; should-fix: the grid overrun above), PASS (62; three minors, all fixed: the stats' columns, a comment that said "row" for "grid", the record column). - **Choices taken.** The turn and the growth are one move (from -3 degrees at 1 to +3 at 1.06), so a drawing is smallest at one end of its sweep; phases in a stride order, so no two neighbours start together. The overscan is computed per frame from the tokens by size containers, not a fixed margin, so a tall layout and a wide one each get exactly what the turn needs; a patch has no overscan (its frame is inside a tile), its rings kept clear of the frame at every pose instead. A course home's hero keeps its own height and its topography runs under the stats and buttons, whose boxes are opaque. A history course's topography, like its tile's patch on the home, leaves out the audit unit (a review, not a unit of reading); on the course home the audit unit keeps its own tile, pending, its modules flat rings. What's new: a contour every 11,000 words, fixed, so 22 Oct stands 20 levels and the smallest day (30 Oct) one; each day in the tone of the course that filed the most words that day. A markdown unit not yet written links to its brief. The markdown course homes are fetched files.

## 4 November 2026 · Session 57 · The home's hero: a topography from the data, the areas cycling, a quiet What's new (P92)

P92 ran in one chat on Opus 5.5 from 13:24 on 4 November, on Pedro's decisions of 13:16 (P91's shelf "looks awful [...] Such a long scroll in mobile, makes no sense. Let's go back"; after research into 2026 heroes: type first, the action in view, motion beside the headline rather than heavy motion behind it, a reduced-motion fallback for every animation). The clone was at 29c376d; the permission check did not refuse the repo's scripts. Baseline `build.py` and `verify.py`: ALL CHECKS PASSED; the build changed the README size table and the shell's Backlog and Changelog pages (P92's queued row), committed as f155586. The home shots of the start are `reviews/p92/before/` (320, 360, 390, 430, 768 and 1280 px, dark and light, scaled to device scale 2). No unit and no history report's text changed, so the Greece edition is not republished (it builds).

- **The hero (step 2).** "Academy"; under it the four areas of knowledge (the `THEMES` names in order, each in its tone) one at a time, three seconds each, cross-fading, by CSS keyframes on stacked words (opacity and transform, no script, no link or focus stop), aria-hidden; one static line naming all four in their tones for a screen reader (its dots aria-hidden, a comma for the pause), the line shown under reduced motion, when nothing moves; the lede as it was; a quiet outlined link, "What's new · IA7 · 3 Nov" (the first of `dated_units`), 44 px, to `#new`. Behind it the topography: `viz.py`'s new home kind `contours` (P91's `shelf`, its keys, classes and checks gone), declared by `build.py` (`HERO_TOPO`): one rise per area, its height the words written in its live courses (History 510,493, Marketing 74,284, Technology 82,023, User Experience 66,866), a contour every 22,000 words (`HERO_INTERVAL`: 23 levels, 3, 3, 3), in two fixed layouts (`HERO_AT`: tall below 1100 px, every rise inside a 390 px phone's view; wide from 1100 px, across the full width beside the sidebar), each small rise closing two rings of its own; the field a sum of smooth bumps (a Gaussian whose outline two angular harmonics push in and out, phases from the label, so every build draws the same) sampled on a grid, contour lines by marching squares, simplified (Ramer-Douglas-Peucker) and written in whole-unit relative steps, each stretch of line in the tone of the rise that dominates it; aria-hidden, no text, 14,016 bytes. It drifts 8 x 6 px over 30 s by a CSS transform where motion is welcome, is still otherwise, and fades over the hero's last 10%. The hero is `70svh` tall, its copy in the lower part with the contours above it, so on a phone the first area's heading peeks under it; it bleeds to `100cqw` (the body is an inline-size container: the width beside the sidebar less any scrollbar) with its copy in the column. No totals, no shelf, no Continue, no map.
- **The areas (step 3).** The four sections in `THEMES` order; from 768 px the header holds the "Track" label, the name, the lede and the count with its rule, on a phone the name only, closed by the first tile's rule; one tile per live course with the same fields (the chip with the pill under it, the title, the two-line tile, the three stats) and its own static contour patch (`tile_patch`: one rise per unit in course order, a written unit by its words, a planned unit a dashed ring, a contour every 5.5th part of the course's largest unit, 4:1, the tile's width), the whole tile the link. History's two folded blocks stay in its section; the constants, the site links, New since and its one link as P91 left them. Hours of reading take one decimal everywhere (16.4, 5.0), on the tiles and the course homes.
- **Theme 6.5 (step 4).** 4h: `.hhome`, `.hcopy`, `.harea`, `.harea-w`, `.harea-all`, `.harea-dot`, `.hsr`, `.hnew`, `.hnew-a`, the phone area header; 4i: `.topo`, `.topo-l`, `.topo-tall`, `.topo-wide`, `.topo-patch`, `.ct-rise`, `.ct-plan`, `.ct-t1` to `.ct-t6`; 6: the header's label, lede, count and rule from 768 px, the wide layout and the area line at 34 px from 1100 px; 3: `body{container-type:inline-size}`. New tokens `--topo-a` (.2 in both themes) and `--topo-p` (.62 dark, .6 light). The shelf's classes and tokens, `.htop`, `.htot` and the 1100 px full-bleed rule are gone. A closed rule block's sub-label sits beside its + (it floated between the title and the + by their widths) and under its title below 768 px. `contrast.py` measures the hero's pairs in both themes, each over a line of every tone (`reviews/p92/contrast.txt`, all pass); `verify.py` measures every hero text against the pixels under it, lowest 3.31:1 dark and 3.47:1 light for the 28 px area words (3:1 needed), the lede and the link at 4.5:1 and over.
- **`shell.js`.** Unchanged: the hero runs no script, and P91's routing serves the tiles and the link.
- **Checks (step 5).** `verify.py`'s home checks are rewritten for the hero against the sources (`home_expect` gives each area's words and each course's units with their words: a history unit's reports counted from the vault, a markdown unit from its reading page): the area line's names, order and tones, aria-hidden; the static line; the lede; the link; the contour layer (aria-hidden, textless, under 20 KB, full width beside the sidebar, one layout shown, one rise per area with its words, in word order, in its tone, each closing two rings); the hero 60 to 80% of a phone's screen with the first heading inside it; no totals, shelf, Continue or map; the area headers; every tile's fields and its patch (one rise per written unit, its words from the sources, rings in word order); the order; New since; no sideways scroll; at 320, 360, 390, 430, 768 and 1280 px, dark and light, with reduced motion (no animation runs) and with motion allowed (only transform and opacity, the four words and the shown layout, none on a tile; the hero's texts measured at 0, 15 and 30 s of the drift with each word shown). Found and fixed on the way: every "dark" pass before P92 ran in the light theme (Playwright's system setting is light and those passes never set the toggle), so contexts now default to dark; round line caps doubled a line where one rise's stretch handed over to the next (butt caps). `--shots` adds the hero's motion sheets at 390 and 1280 px. LCP 112 ms, no long task, at 390 px with motion allowed on a fresh load from the local server. ALL CHECKS PASSED.
- **The checkers (step 6).** Fresh Opus 5.5 agents (claude-opus-5-5 in every transcript), each given only its folder; transcripts checked by script. The hero checker: PASS on the first (31 calls; minors: the copy adrift in a tall box, the cross-fade the comment claimed did not cross, verify's one lowest ratio, faint dark rises), FIX on the second (30 calls: motion contrast measured at two widths and one moment; the User Experience rise in the fade; Marketing clipped at the sidebar), FIX on the third (31 calls: on phones User Experience closed no ring of its own; minors: Marketing at the sidebar's edge at the drift's end, a dead padding value, the dots read aloud), PASS on the fourth (39 calls; minors: the What's new link's dots read aloud, a contour line through the transparent link, verify's rows at 390, 768 and 1280 px without their width, the static line's middle line alone on a narrow phone). The layout checker: PASS on the first (57 calls; minors: a course named two ways, mixed capitals, History's count of reports, the 430 px rule-block summary, ring counts against words, two parallel rules on a phone), FIX on the second (59 calls: the rule blocks' sub-labels floating from 768 px; its transcript held one Bash call, `true`, a no-op that read nothing, against its brief, and the next brief forbade it by name), PASS on the third (57 calls; minor: hours written two ways). Every finding was fixed in `theme.css`, `viz.py`, `build.py` or `verify.py`, or settled as a given in the next brief: the hero's height and lede are Pedro's ("about 70% of the screen", "the lede as it is"), the lines are faint by the row's rule ("a low opacity that leaves every text on the hero at its contrast"), the course names and History's count are as their courses and P90 and P91 set them, the light bands are P90's ("the light theme's paper and accents as P90 set them"). The passing reports' minors were fixed after them (the link's dots aria-hidden with a comma for a screen reader, as the static line's; every hero contrast row names its width; hours of reading with one decimal everywhere) or settled with a reason (a line through the link: a plate behind it would fill it, against Pedro's "not a filled button", and its text measures 9.57:1 and its arrow 5.09:1 over the lines; four area names at 28 px cannot share two lines in a 288 to 358 px column, so the static line balances to three there). Reports verbatim at `reviews/p92/` (`check-hero-1` to `-4`, `check-layout-1` to `-3`), with `contrast.txt`, `verify-after.txt`, the before and after shots and the motion sheets.
- **Choices taken.** Two fixed layouts in one layer, not one cropped drawing (a square drawing cropped for a phone left the desktop's sides bare, a wide one cropped the phone's rises off); a contour every 22,000 words, fixed, so a rise grows with its words rather than every area keeping three rings; a rise's spread grows with the cube root of its words; a tile's patch steps by its own largest unit, so every patch reads at one density (the tile's numbers give the size); the static line, not a sentence, is what a screen reader reads; the copy sits low in the hero; the body is the container the bleed is measured against (no `100vw`, which counts a scrollbar); hours of reading take one decimal everywhere.
- **Published.** The Academy artifact, read first: the live shell was P91's v125, identical line for line to the start build (f155586) apart from the hosting wrapper and the Changelog and Backlog lines P91's and P92's records added after it. The Project copies `claude/BACKLOG-chats.md`, `claude/CHANGELOG-chats.md`, `theme.css`, `design-README.md` and `claude/academy.md` re-uploaded with the Projects tool.
